It's just been occupying a lot of my thoughts, so I figured I should probably talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually high, euphoric, or especially energized lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. Most days lately I’ve felt unusually switched on and really optimistic... kind of like I’m right on the edge of something huge. But at the same time, I've noticed I'm also getting frustrated pretty easily. Like, I'll just snap at my teammates, or my boyfriend, over small delays that normally wouldn’t bother me at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you been needing much less sleep than usual and still feeling full of energy?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I'm still sleeping about my normal amount... probably seven or eight hours a night. Even though I feel really energized during the day, I haven't actually noticed needing less sleep or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: When you notice these signals, do you ever actually hear a voice speaking to you when no one is there?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually. Um, a few times a week, I do. It usually happens when I'm working late, like on school stuff or operations plans for work. I'll hear my name, or just a short phrase like \"go now.\" It sounds so clear that I actually check my phone or look around the room, even though I'm totally alone.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe you have a special talent, power, or importance that explains why these people are sending you these signals?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. I mean, I don't think I have any kind of special power or extreme importance. I'm just a regular person trying to manage my job and my life. I really don't know why I'm hearing it, to be honest, but I definitely don't think it's because I'm chosen or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: When these beliefs and voices first started, were you already feeling unusually energized or irritable?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they definitely started around the same time. Before this whole stretch of feeling so energized and driven, I never had messages feel coded to me, and I definitely wasn't hearing my name when no one was around. All of that really only started happening once my mood shifted and my workload got so intense, right after I started taking those MBA prerequisites.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these messages mean you’re being selected for an unusually important role or success?\n\nPatient: No, I don't. Like I mentioned a minute ago, I really don't feel like I'm chosen for anything special. So, no, I don't think these messages mean I'm being selected for some huge role or massive success or anything like that. It's just... it's just happening, but I don't attach that kind of grand meaning to it. I'm really just focused on my normal job and my classes.\n\nDoctor: Have there been at least two weeks when you were hearing the voice or noticing the coded messages without also feeling unusually energized or irritable?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. Like I was saying before, the voices and the coded messages... they really only started happening after I began those MBA prerequisites and my mood and workload got so intense. Before this whole stretch of feeling so energized, I never experienced anything like that. So, no, there hasn't been a time where I had those experiences without also feeling this way.\n\nDoctor: How long has this period of feeling unusually energized and irritable been going on?\n\nPatient: It's been about two months now. Yeah, it pretty much started right when I began the part-time MBA prerequisite program... while still keeping up with my regular operations job. So, about two months.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hospitalized because of these experiences?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't been hospitalized. Honestly, I haven't even seen a doctor about any of this until now. I've just been pushing through it with work and school.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. All of that really only started happening once my mood shifted and my workload got so intense, right after I started taking those MBA prerequisites.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these messages mean you’re being selected for an unusually important role or success?\n\nPatient: No, I don't. Like I mentioned a minute ago, I really don't feel like I'm chosen for anything special. So, no, I don't think these messages mean I'm being selected for some huge role or massive success or anything like that. It's just... it's just happening, but I don't attach that kind of grand meaning to it. I'm really just focused on my normal job and my classes.\n\nDoctor: Have there been at least two weeks when you were hearing the voice or noticing the coded messages without also feeling unusually energized or irritable?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. Like I was saying before, the voices and the coded messages... they really only started happening after I began those MBA prerequisites and my mood and workload got so intense. Before this whole stretch of feeling so energized, I never experienced anything like that. So, no, there hasn't been a time where I had those experiences without also feeling this way.\n\nDoctor: How long has this period of feeling unusually energized and irritable been going on?\n\nPatient: It's been about two months now. Yeah, it pretty much started right when I began the part-time MBA prerequisite program... while still keeping up with my regular operations job. So, about two months.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hospitalized because of these experiences?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't been hospitalized. Honestly, I haven't even seen a doctor about any of this until now. I've just been pushing through it with work and school.\n\nDoctor: Have you been more driven than usual to start projects or get things done?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I’ve been launching extra work projects at my job, and... well, rewriting team processes for my classes. I've also been messaging a bunch of contacts from networking events and trying to organize everyone’s schedules. It just feels like I need to be doing all of it right now. Though, honestly, taking all that extra stuff on has actually made me miss the normal follow-through on my actual day-to-day tasks.\n\nDoctor: Has this period made it hard for you to function normally at work or in school?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has. Um, my manager actually started documenting some missed handoffs and just... unreliable follow-through on my end. And in my program, my professor questioned my judgment on a recent group assignment. I mean, I'm still showing up to everything, but my relationships at work and school are getting pretty strained. That's honestly why I felt like I needed to come in and get some help.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any time during this period when you needed to be hospitalized for your mental state?\n\nPatient: No, like I mentioned earlier, I haven't been hospitalized at all. This is my first time seeing a doctor for any of this.\n\nDoctor: During this period, have you ever become so out of touch with reality that someone else felt you needed emergency psychiatric care?\n\nPatient: No, no one has ever suggested that. Um, I mean, people can definitely tell I'm acting differently and taking on too much, but nobody has thought I was out of touch with reality or needed emergency care. It hasn't been anything extreme like that.\n\nDoctor: Before these past two months, have you ever had another period when you felt unusually energized or irritable like this?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. Um, looking back, I can't really remember ever feeling quite like this before. This whole thing over the last couple of months feels pretty new to me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there.
